=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given a permutation P of (1,2,\dots,N) and an integer K.  
Find the expected value, modulo 998244353, of the inversion number of P after performing the following operation:

- First, choose an integer i uniformly at random between 1 and N - K + 1, inclusive.
- Then, shuffle P_i, P_{i+1}, \dots, P_{i+K-1} uniformly at random.


What is the inversion number?
The inversion number of a sequence (A_1, A_2, \dots, A_N) is the number of integer pairs (i, j) satisfying 1 \le i < j \le N and A_i > A_j.


What does "expected value modulo 998244353" mean?
It can be proved that the sought expected value is always rational. Under the constraints of this problem, when this value is represented as an irreducible fraction \frac{P}{Q}, it can also be proved that Q \not\equiv 0 \pmod{998244353}. Thus, there is a unique integer R satisfying R \times Q \equiv P \pmod{998244353}, \ 0 \le R < 998244353. Report this integer R.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N K
P_1 P_2 \dots P_N

Output

Print the answer in one line.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 1 \le K \le N \le 2 \times 10^5
- P is a permutation of (1,2,\dots,N).

Sample Input 1

4 2
1 4 2 3

Sample Output 1

166374061

The operation changes the permutation P into the following:

- (1,4,2,3) ... probability 1/2
- (4,1,2,3) ... probability 1/6
- (1,2,4,3) ... probability 1/6
- (1,4,3,2) ... probability 1/6

The expected value of the inversion number is \displaystyle 2 \times \frac{1}{2} + 3 \times \frac{1}{6} + 1 \times \frac{1}{6} + 3 \times \frac{1}{6} = \frac{13}{6}.
\displaystyle \frac{13}{6} modulo 998244353 is 166374061, so print this number.

Sample Input 2

1 1
1

Sample Output 2

0

Sample Input 3

10 6
7 4 10 5 6 1 8 2 3 9

Sample Output 3

499122200

- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Let's verify cross_expect update carefully with example.

Define for current interval [l,r], S = values in positions l..r.
left outside L = positions < l; right outside R = positions > r.
cross(l) = sum_{p in L} cnt_S(<P_p) + sum_{p in R} cnt_S(>P_p).

Let F_l(v) = # {p in L: P_p > v} + # {p in R: P_p < v}. Then cross(l) = sum_{v in S} F_l(v). Because sum_{p in L} cnt_S(<P_p) = sum_{v in S} # {p in L: P_p > v}. Yes.

When move to l+1:
L' = L ∪ {x} where x=l, value a=P_l.
R' = R \ {y} where y=r+1=l+K, value b=P_y.
S' = S \ {a} ∪ {b}.

For any v:
F'(v) = #L' > v + #R' < v
= (#L > v + [a>v]) + (#R < v - [b<v]) = F(v) + [a>v] - [b<v].

Then cross' = sum_{v in S'} F'(v)
= sum_{v in S'} F(v) + sum_{v in S'} [a>v] - sum_{v in S'} [b<v].

sum_{v in S'} F(v) = sum_{v in S} F(v) - F(a) + F(b) (since S' = S - {a} + {b}; a,b distinct).
= cross - F(a) + F(b).

sum_{v in S'} [a>v] = number of v in S' with v < a. Since values distinct, a not in S'. Good.
sum_{v in S'} [b<v] = number of v in S' with v > b.
So cross' = cross - F(a) + F(b) + count_S'(<a) - count_S'(>b).

Need compute F(a), F(b) before changing L/R/S? F uses current L,R. We can query before updates. Good.

But note when we update state for next interval, we need S' for count_S' queries. We can compute count_S'(<a) and count_S'(>b) by updating S Fenwick first? Or using current S:
S' = S - {a} + {b}. count_S'(<a) = count_S(<a) - [a<a?0] + [b<a] = count_S(<a) + [b<a]. Since b may be <a. count_S'(>b) = count_S(>b) - [a>b] + [b>b?0] = count_S(>b) - [a>b]. But easier update S Fenwick to S' then query. Need careful if we need F(a),F(b) before boundary updates; S update independent. We can:
- Query F(a), F(b) using current L,R.
- Query current S maybe for count_S'(<a), count_S'(>b) after updating S? We can update S Fenwick remove a add b, then query count <a and >b. But if we update S before computing cross? cross already known. F queries unaffected by S. So okay.
- Then update L/R Fenwicks and inv_outside.
- Compute cross' using formula. Need F(a), F(b) from old L/R. If we update L/R before computing cross', we need saved F_a,F_b. So save.

Potential pitfalls:
- Fenwick sum(a) with a=0? Values 1..N. Need handle sum(0)=0. count_greater(v) = total - sum(v) because values distinct and v may or may not in tree. If v in tree, sum(v) includes v, total - sum(v) counts >v. If v not in tree, sum(v) counts <=v, same as <v, total - sum(v) counts >v. Good.
- count_less(v) = sum(v-1).
- For S.count_greater_b = K - S.sum(b) because S size K and b in S after update. If b in S, sum(b) includes b and all <b, so K-sum(b) counts >b. Good.
- For count_less_a = S.sum(a-1), a not in S after update, so counts <a. Good.
- For cross_init, S contains P[0..K-1]. For p outside, P[p] not in S. count_greater = K - S.sum(P[p])? Since P[p] not in S, sum(P[p]) counts values <P[p] (not equal), K - sum(P[p]) counts >P[p]. If use less = S.sum(P[p]-1), K-less also >. Both same because no equal. Use K - S.sum(P[p]) maybe. But if P[p] not in S, sum(P[p]) = sum(P[p]-1). Use S.sum(P[p]) to avoid off by? If P[p] not in S, okay. But if K? We'll use K - S.sum(P[p]) for count greater. Since no equal. If P[p] could be in S? For p>=K, not in S because permutation positions disjoint. Yes.
- For S Fenwick update: remove a then add b. Since a,b distinct. If K=1? Let's examine K=1. Operation chooses length 1 interval, shuffle no change. Expected inversion = original T. Our formulas?
K=1, M=N. C=0. For any interval length1, shuffle single element no change. inv_outside(l) = inversions outside position l. cross_expect(l)? S={P_l}. For left outside p<l, cnt_S(<P_p) = [P_l < P_p] (inversion p,l). For right outside p>l, cnt_S(>P_p) = [P_l > P_p] (inversion l,p). So cross_expect(l) = number of inversions involving position l. inv_outside(l)+cross_expect(l)=T. Sum over l = M*T? Wait each interval length1, E_l=T. Sum E_l = N*T. Our formula sum_inv+sum_cross should equal N*T. Does algorithm handle K=1?
Let's test K=1. M=N. init l=0 interval [0,0], S={P0}, right outside positions 1..N-1. inv_outside = inversions among suffix positions 1..N-1. cross_init = sum_{p>0} cnt_S(>P_p) = # inversions with position0 as left? Fenwick implementation optimized:
class Fenwick:
    def __init__(self,n):
        self.n=n; self.bit=[0]*(n+1)
    def add(self,i,delta):
        n=self.n; bit=self.bit
        while i<=n:
            bit[i]+=delta; i+=i&-i
    def sum(self,i):
        s=0; bit=self.bit
        while i>0:
            s+=bit[i]; i-=i&-i
        return s
Method calls overhead maybe okay but can inline? For performance, define local functions? Python method calls inside 2e5* many maybe okay but could be ~1.2M method calls, okay. Actually sum calls ~6*2e5=1.2M, add calls ~4*2e5=0.8M, total 2M method calls, fine. While iterations dominate.

Let's think about using arrays and local functions for Fenwick:
def bit_add(bit, i, x):
    while i <= N:
        bit[i] += x
        i += i & -i
def bit_sum(bit, i):
    s = 0
    while i > 0:
        s += bit[i]
        i -= i & -i
    return s

import sys
MOD=998244353
INV4=pow(4, MOD-2, MOD) maybe constant 748683265? 4 inverse mod 998244353 = 748683265? Let's compute: 998244353+1 /4? 998244353 mod4=1, inverse 4 = (3*MOD+1)/4? class Fenwick:
    __slots__ = ('n','bit')
    def __init__(self,n):
       self.n=n; self.bit=[0]*(n+1)
    def add(self,i,x):
       n=self.n; bit=self.bit
       while i<=n:
          bit[i]+=x; i += i & -i
    def sum(self,i):
       s=0; bit=self.bit
       while i>0:
          s += bit[i]; i -= i & -i
       return s

def main():
 data=list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
 N,K=data[0],data[1]; P=data[2:]
 M=N-K+1
 left=Fenwick(N); right=Fenwick(N); S=Fenwick(N)
 # build S
 for v in P[:K]: S.add(v,1)  # slicing creates list; avoid? P[:K] copy maybe okay but can avoid.
 for idx in range(K): S.add(P[idx],1)
 # build right and inv_out
 inv_out=0
 for p in range(N-1, K-1, -1):
     v=P[p]
     inv_out += right.sum(v-1)
     right.add(v,1)
 # cross_init
 cross=0
 for p in range(K,N):
     v=P[p]
     less = S.sum(v-1)
     cross += K - less
 sum_inv = inv_out
 sum_cross = cross
 # bind methods
 left_sum=left.sum; left_add=left.add
 right_sum=right.sum; right_add=right.add
 S_sum=S.sum; S_add=S.add
 for l in range(M-1):
     a = P[l]
     b = P[l+K]
     # F_a
     left_gt_a = l - left_sum(a)  # left_total = l
     right_lt_a = right_sum(a-1)
     F_a = left_gt_a + right_lt_a
     # F_b
     left_gt_b = l - left_sum(b)
     right_lt_b = right_sum(b-1)
     F_b = left_gt_b + right_lt_b
     # update inv_out
     inv_out -= F_b
     right_add(b, -1)
     inv_out += F_a - (1 if b < a else 0)
     left_add(a, 1)
     # update S
     S_add(a, -1)
     S_add(b, 1)
     # update cross
     cnt_less_a = S_sum(a-1)
     cnt_greater_b = K - S_sum(b)
     cross = cross - F_a + F_b + cnt_less_a - cnt_greater_b
     sum_inv += inv_out
     sum_cross += cross
 C = K*(K-1) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
 ans = ( (sum_inv + sum_cross) % MOD * pow(M, MOD-2, MOD) + C ) % MOD
 print(ans)

Let's maybe derive inv_outside update formula in terms of F_b and F_a to ensure no missing pair between y and x? When moving from old outside to new outside:
Old outside O = L ∪ R (R includes y).
New outside O' = (L ∪ {x}) ∪ (R\{y}).
We want inv(O') = inv(O) - contrib(y, O) + contrib(x, O').
Where contrib(y,O) counts inversions between y and any z in O. This is F_b? Let's verify F_b = #L > b + #R < b. For z in O:
- If z in L, z<y always. Pair (z,y) inversion if P_z > P_y = b. Count L > b.
- If z in R, z>=y. If z=y, not pair. If z>y, pair (y,z) inversion if b > P_z. Count R < b (excluding y because b<b false). So F_b. Good.
contrib(x,O') = # z in O' with inversion involving x. O' = L ∪ (R\{y}). z in L: z<x always. Pair (z,x) inversion if P_z > a. Count L > a. z in R\{y}: z>x always. Pair (x,z) inversion if a > P_z. Count (R\{y}) < a. This equals left.greater(a) + right_new.less(a) = F_a - (b<a). Because F_a = L>a + R_old<a. R_old<a includes b if b<a. R_new<a = R_old<a - [b<a]. Good. So inv update correct.

Now, cross update formula maybe can also be derived with F. Let's verify F(v) definition with current L/R:
F(v) = L_gt(v) + R_lt(v).
cross = sum_{v in S} F(v). We should prove:
sum_{p in L} cnt_S(<P_p) = sum_{p in L} #{v in S: v < P_p} = sum_{v in S} #{p in L: P_p > v} = sum_{v in S} L_gt(v).
sum_{p in R} cnt_S(>P_p) = sum_{p in R} #{v in S: v > P_p} = sum_{v in S} #{p in R: P_p < v} = sum_{v in S} R_lt(v). Good.

Now, update F:
L' = L ∪ {x}, R' = R\{y}. For any v:
L'_gt(v) = L_gt(v) + [a > v].
R'_lt(v) = R_lt(v) - [b < v] (since y removed; if b<v then y was counted in R_lt(v), else not).
So F'(v) = F(v) + [a>v] - [b<v].
Then cross' = sum_{v in S'} F'(v) = sum_{v in S'} F(v) + A - B.
A = # {v in S': a > v} = count_S'(<a).
B = # {v in S': b < v} = count_S'(>b).
sum_{v in S'} F(v) = sum_{v in S} F(v) - F(a) + F(b) because S' = S - {a} + {b}. Since a,b distinct. Good.
So formula exact.

Full code:

import sys

MOD = 998244353
INV4 = 748683265

class Fenwick:
    __slots__ = ('n','bit')
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.bit = [0] * (n + 1)
    def add(self, i, x):
        n = self.n
        bit = self.bit
        while i <= n:
            bit[i] += x
            i += i & -i
    def sum(self, i):
        s = 0
        bit = self.bit
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        return s

def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return
    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    P = data[2:]
    M = N - K + 1

    left = Fenwick(N)
    right = Fenwick(N)
    seg = Fenwick(N)

    for i in range(K):
        seg.add(P[i], 1)

    inv_out = 0
    for i in range(N - 1, K - 1, -1):
        v = P[i]
        inv_out += right.sum(v - 1)
        right.add(v, 1)

    cross = 0
    for i in range(K, N):
        v = P[i]
        less = seg.sum(v - 1)
        cross += K - less

    sum_inv = inv_out
    sum_cross = cross

    left_sum = left.sum
    left_add = left.add
    right_sum = right.sum
    right_add = right.add
    seg_sum = seg.sum
    seg_add = seg.add

    for l in range(M - 1):
        a = P[l]
        b = P[l + K]

        # F(a), F(b) with current outside sets
        left_gt_a = l - left_sum(a)
        right_lt_a = right_sum(a - 1)
        Fa = left_gt_a + right_lt_a

        left_gt_b = l - left_sum(b)
        right_lt_b = right_sum(b - 1)
        Fb = left_gt_b + right_lt_b

        # move interval: right outside loses b, left outside gains a
        inv_out -= Fb
        right_add(b, -1)
        inv_out += Fa - (1 if b < a else 0)
        left_add(a, 1)

        # update interval value set
        seg_add(a, -1)
        seg_add(b, 1)

        # update cross expectation
        cnt_less_a = seg_sum(a - 1)
        cnt_greater_b = K - seg_sum(b)
        cross = cross - Fa + Fb + cnt_less_a - cnt_greater_b

        sum_inv += inv_out
        sum_cross += cross

    c = K * (K - 1) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
    ans = ((sum_inv + sum_cross) % MOD) * pow(M, MOD - 2, MOD) % MOD
    ans = (ans + c) % MOD
    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "inv_out += Fa - (1 if b < a else 0)" after right_add(b,-1). If b<a, we subtract one. If b>a, no. What if b<a but b was not in right? It is. Good.
Could write `inv_out += Fa - (b < a)`
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

MOD = 998244353
INV4 = 748683265


class Fenwick:
    __slots__ = ("n", "bit")

    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.bit = [0] * (n + 1)

    def add(self, i, x):
        n = self.n
        bit = self.bit
        while i <= n:
            bit[i] += x
            i += i & -i

    def sum(self, i):
        s = 0
        bit = self.bit
        while i > 0:
            s += bit[i]
            i -= i & -i
        return s


def main():
    data = list(map(int, sys.stdin.buffer.read().split()))
    if not data:
        return

    N, K = data[0], data[1]
    P = data[2:]
    M = N - K + 1

    left = Fenwick(N)
    right = Fenwick(N)
    seg = Fenwick(N)

    for i in range(K):
        seg.add(P[i], 1)

    # Inversions among the initial right outside part, and build right Fenwick.
    inv_out = 0
    for i in range(N - 1, K - 1, -1):
        v = P[i]
        inv_out += right.sum(v - 1)
        right.add(v, 1)

    # Cross expectation for the first interval [0, K-1].
    cross = 0
    for i in range(K, N):
        v = P[i]
        less = seg.sum(v - 1)
        cross += K - less

    sum_inv = inv_out
    sum_cross = cross

    left_sum = left.sum
    left_add = left.add
    right_sum = right.sum
    right_add = right.add
    seg_sum = seg.sum
    seg_add = seg.add

    for l in range(M - 1):
        a = P[l]
        b = P[l + K]

        # F(v) = (# left outside values > v) + (# right outside values < v)
        left_gt_a = l - left_sum(a)
        right_lt_a = right_sum(a - 1)
        Fa = left_gt_a + right_lt_a

        left_gt_b = l - left_sum(b)
        right_lt_b = right_sum(b - 1)
        Fb = left_gt_b + right_lt_b

        # Move interval right: right outside loses b, left outside gains a.
        inv_out -= Fb
        right_add(b, -1)
        inv_out += Fa - (1 if b < a else 0)
        left_add(a, 1)

        # Update the value set of the interval.
        seg_add(a, -1)
        seg_add(b, 1)

        # Update cross expectation.
        cnt_less_a = seg_sum(a - 1)
        cnt_greater_b = K - seg_sum(b)
        cross = cross - Fa + Fb + cnt_less_a - cnt_greater_b

        sum_inv += inv_out
        sum_cross += cross

    inside_expected = K * (K - 1) % MOD * INV4 % MOD
    ans = ((sum_inv + sum_cross) % MOD) * pow(M, MOD - 2, MOD) % MOD
    ans = (ans + inside_expected) % MOD

    print(ans)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
